## Document 011: 10月金融数据点评

### Page 1

交易咨询业务资格：证监许可【2012】31号

10月金融数据点评

宏观 
报告日期 2026—11-16 专题报告 
   10月金融数据呈现"社融微升、信贷负增、M1 修复、M2续
降"的组合。社融存量同比增速较 9 月回升 0.1 个百分点
至7.2%，主要得益于政府债券集中发行；单月新增社融约
1.15 万亿元，同比多增逾 3000 亿元，但增量几乎全部由
政府部门贡献。金融机构新增人民币贷款-1000 亿元，同
比少增约 3200 亿元，票据融资退潮是主要拖累，企业中
长期贷款边际改善但居民端连续净偿还。货币端 M1 同比
回升至 4.4%、M2 同比降至 7.1%，剪刀差收敛至 2.7 个百
分点，资金活化出现阶段性修复信号，但其中包含低基数
因素，尚不能确认企业内生扩张重启。综合来看，10月数
据延续了"降速提质"的存量验证逻辑：财政托底使总量读
数企稳，但私人部门融资需求仍偏弱，政策重心正由"宽
松力度"转向"传导效率"。

国贸期货  樊梦真 
从业资格证号： F3035483 
投资咨询证号： Z0014706

### Page 2

交易咨询业务资格：证监许可【2012】31号

➢ 事件： 
2026 年 11 月 13 日，中国人民银行发布《2026 年 10 月金融统计数据报告》。10 月末社会融资规模存
量 469.16 万亿元，同比增长 7.2%（前值为 7.1%）；前十个月社融增量累计 28.61 万亿元，比上年同期少
2.29 万亿元，10 月单月新增约 1.15 万亿元，同比多增逾 3000 亿元。信贷方面，10 月末人民币贷款余额
283.33万亿元，同比增长4.7%（前值4.8%）；前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元，10月单月少增1000
亿元，同比少增约3200亿元。10 月末 M2余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%（前值7.2%）； M1余额116.9
万亿元，同比增长 4.4%（前值3.2%）。 
图表 1：社融和信贷  图表 2：M1和 M2

资料来源：Wind、国贸期货研究院  资料来源：Wind、国贸期货研究院

➢ 点评： 
 总量：社融增速止跌微升，但改善几乎全部来自政府债单极支撑 
10 月社融存量同比 7.2%，结束此前连续两个月的回落，单月新增社融约 1.15 万亿元，同比多增逾
3000亿元。从结构归因看，改善动力高度集中于政府债券：10月政府债净融资约1.18-1.19万亿元，占当
月社融增量比重超过100%，剔除政府债后的社融增速降至约5.2%，私人部门融资需求仍在走弱。前十个月
直接融资合计占社融增量约 53%，较前三季度进一步提升，超过贷款占比约 14 个百分点，融资结构由信贷
主导转向"政府债+直接融资"双支柱的格局进一步固化。从资金价格信号看，10 月企业新发放贷款加权平
均利率约2.9%，同比低约0.2个百分点；个人住房贷款利率约3.1%，与上年同期基本持平，金融供给端条
件仍处历史宽松区间。因此，总量读数的企稳更多反映财政发力和直接融资渠道畅通，而非实体自发融资

### Page 3

交易咨询业务资格：证监许可【2012】31号

需求的反转。 
图表 3：社融的季节性  图表 4：社融&M2&资金价格

资料来源：Wind、国贸期货研究院  资料来源：Wind、国贸期货研究院

 结构：政府债独撑，企业债修复、非标分化 
10 月社融分项呈"政府债独撑、信贷拖累、直接融资补位"格局。政府债券净融资约 1.18 万亿元、同
比多增约 7000 亿元，是社融同比多增的唯一正向来源；社融口径人民币贷款减少约 5800 亿元、同比多减
约 5700 亿元，是最主要拖累。直接融资方面，企业债券净融资约 3400 亿元、同比多增约 950 亿元，在债
券利率低位环境下，债券对信贷的替代效应继续显现；股票融资约 350 亿元、同比略少增。表外融资边际
改善：委托贷款增加约1860亿元、同比多增约200亿元，与政策性金融工具通过委托贷款通道投放有关；
未贴现银行承兑汇票减少约 1700 亿元但同比少减约 1200 亿元；信托贷款减少约 415 亿元。综合看，10 月
社融的结构特征可概括为"财政补位、票据退潮"——政府信用扩张对冲了银行表内信贷的季节性收缩，但
直接融资对信贷的替代并非线性推进，单月节奏仍存在波动。

图表 5：社融的细分结构  图表 6：地方政府债券

资料来源：Wind、国贸期货研究院  资料来源：Wind、国贸期货研究院

 信贷：票据退潮拖累单月负增，企业中长期边际修复、居民持续收缩 
10 月新增人民币贷款-1000 亿元，为继 4 月、7 月之后年内第三次单月负增，同比少增约 3200 亿元。
结构拆解显示，票据融资同比少增约8000亿元，是信贷负增的主因——前期集中投放的票据陆续到期，叠
加监管对票据冲量约束强化，银行"以票充贷"空间明显压缩。剔除票据后，企业贷款实际新增约 1500 亿
元、同比多增约3000亿元，其中企业中长期贷款新增约1600亿元、同比多增约1300亿元，可能初步体现
新型政策性金融工具与专项债配套的融资支持，但改善幅度有限、持续性仍待观察。 
居民端则延续净偿还：10 月住户贷款减少约 4300 亿元，同比多减约 700 亿元；短期贷款减少约 2700
亿元，中长期贷款减少约 1560 亿元、同比多减约 900 亿元。前三季度累计住户贷款已减少 1.03 万亿元，
10 月单月再减约 4300 亿元，前 10 月累计减少 1.46万亿元，而去年同期为净增加 1.10万亿元，居民部门
去杠杆已从慢变量演化为持续性硬读数，与 10月 30城商品房成交面积同比降幅扩大至 18%相互印证。

 货币供应：M1 回升至4.4%、M2 降至 7.1% 
10 月末 M1 同比 4.4%，较 9 月回升1.2 个百分点；M2同比7.1%，较 9月再降 0.3个百分点；M2-M1 剪
刀差由 4.0 个百分点收窄至 2.7 个百分点，为年内较优水平。M1 的回升包含两方面因素：一方面，去年同
期基数偏低对读数形成支撑，不能完全归因为企业经营活跃度实质改善；另一方面，财政资金拨付提速、
企业活期存款边际回流、以及政策性金融工具落地带来的项目资金活化，确实对 M1 形成正向拉动。M2 继
续回落则与信贷派生偏弱、非银存款波动、财政存款淤积有关——10月财政性存款前十个月累计增加3.03
万亿元，政府债募集资金属阶段性滞留国库，尚未完全转化为一般性存款。 
存款结构显示，住户存款前十个月增加 8.47 万亿元，居民端储蓄倾向仍强；企业存款增加 1.91 万亿
元，活化程度边际改善但仍偏谨慎。综合判断，M1 的修复是积极信号，但需结合后续订单、PPI 与项目开

### Page 7

交易咨询业务资格：证监许可【2012】31号

分析师介绍 
樊梦真：国贸期货宏观金融首席分析师，毕业于中国科学院数学与系统科学研究院。国债、外汇研究员，
专注于宏观金融领域的研究，善于利用跨专业背景对宏观经济、外汇、利率及其衍生品进行分析，熟练运
用数理工具为客户提供专业服务与交易策略。曾获期货日报“最佳宏观金融期货分析师”称号。

发布日期 2026/11/16

四、前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元
10月末，本外币存款余额358.81万亿元，同比增长7.8%。月末人民币存款余额350.65万亿元，同比增长7.7%。前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元。其中，住户存款增加8.47万亿元，非金融企业存款增加1.91万亿元，财政性存款增加3.03万亿元，非银行业金融机构存款
增加5.99万亿元。10月末，外币存款余额1.22万亿美元，同比增长16.9%。前十个月外币存款增加1553亿美元。
五、前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元
10月末，本外币贷款余额287.25万亿元，同比增长4.6%。月末人民币贷款余额283.33万亿元，同比增长4.7%。前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元。分部门看，住户贷款减少1.46万亿元，其中，短期贷款减少1.41万亿元，中长期贷款减少501亿元；企（事）业单位贷款
增加12.39万亿元，其中，短期贷款增加4.65万亿元，中长期贷款增加6.69万亿元，票据融资增加9283亿元；非银行业金融机构贷款减少2395亿元。10月末，外币贷款余额5831亿美元，同比增长5.2%。前十个月外币贷款增加381亿美元。
六、10月份银行间人民币市场同业拆借月加权平均利率为1.3%，质押式债券回购月加权平均利率为1.33%
10月份银行间人民币市场以拆借、现券和回购方式合计成交167.98万亿元，日均成交9.33万亿元，日均成交同比增长1.9%。其中，同业拆借日均成交同比下降10.8%，现券日均成交同比增长4.7%，质押式回购日均成交同比增长1.3%。10月份同业拆借加权平均利率为1.3%，
比上月低0.1个百分点，比上年同期低0.09个百分点；质押式回购加权平均利率为1.33%，比上月低0.07个百分点，比上年同期低0.07个百分点。
七、10月份经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元
10月份，经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，其中货物贸易、服务贸易及其他经常项目分别为1.43万亿元、0.47万亿元；直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元，其中对外直接投资、外商直接投资分别为0.21万亿元、0.35万亿元。

中国:M2:同比
2026年10月金融数据

2026年11月13日，人民银行发布2026年1-10月金融统计数据报告。数据显示，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长
4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。
一、社会融资规模存量同比增长7.2%
初步统计，2026年10月末社会融资规模存量为469.16万亿元，同比增长7.2%。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款余额279.31万亿元，同比增长4.6%；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币余额1.26万亿元，同比增长9%；委托贷款余额11.53万亿元，同比增长1.7%；信托
贷款余额4.49万亿元，同比下降0.6%；未贴现的银行承兑汇票余额2.05万亿元，同比下降4.4%；企业债券余额36.92万亿元，同比增长9.6%；政府债券余额106.41万亿元，同比增长14.4%；非金融企业境内股票余额12.77万亿元，同比增长5.5%。
二、前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元
初步统计，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款增加10.91万亿元，同比少增3.61万亿元；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币增加2546亿元，同比多增3692亿元；委托贷款增加2084亿
元，同比多增1002亿元；信托贷款减少1798亿元，同比多减3958亿元；未贴现的银行承兑汇票减少939亿元，同比多减1055亿元；企业债券净融资3.1万亿元，同比多1.27万亿元；政府债券净融资11.49万亿元，同比少4592亿元；非金融企业境内股票融资5705亿元，同比多
1842亿元。
三、广义货币增长7.1%
10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。

## Document 092: 2026年6月投資月報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年6月投資月報.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資月報)
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
美股擺脫通膨疑慮，利率仍偏震盪格局
2026 年 6 月
5 月截至 22 日為止，全球股市表現(以美元計價)呈現高度分化，「AI 半導體」與「油價地緣政
治」兩股力道拉鋸，使各區域報酬差距明顯擴大。MSCI 新興亞洲與新興市場期間分別勁揚 7.1%與
5.4%，雙雙領先，主要受惠台灣與南韓在 AI 半導體供應鏈的強勁買盤，南韓今年漲幅已逾八成、
台灣約四成，為全球最熱的股市。成熟市場方面，標普 500 期間上漲 3.8%，MSCI 日本、歐洲分別
上漲 3%與 1.8%，均有不錯表現。相對地，MSCI 拉丁美洲單月下跌 4.3%，為主要市場中表現最弱，

主因年初大漲後，通膨上揚壓縮巴西後續降息空間及該國的大選選情變化導致投資人獲利了結。中國
與印度表現不佳，大致是受美伊衝突帶動國際油價走高的拖累。
相較於 4 月在美元走弱帶動下的全面反彈，5 月截至 22 日為止，全球債市明顯承壓，七大類債
券中六類收黑，主要仍是因美伊衝突推升國際油價，再度點燃通膨疑慮，全球債券殖利率走升所致。
以券種觀察，存續期間較長、利率敏感度較高的全球主權債券單月下跌 0.85%、全球投資級公司債
也下跌 0.47%；新興當地債亦由４月的領漲轉為小幅回檔，反映美元自５月中旬開始轉強，導致匯
率貢獻由正轉負。亞洲高收益債則逆勢上漲 0.33%，為唯一收紅券種，主要受惠較高的票息收益
（carry）與相對較短的存續期間，於殖利率走升環境中提供緩衝。整體而言，在地緣與通膨變數明
朗化之前，債市配置宜留意存續期間風險以強化防禦性。
儘管中東戰事推升通膨疑慮，但自 4 月起以來，原油、美股走向明顯分歧。美股在 AI 資本支出
題材與強勁財報帶動下改寫歷史新高，與過去地緣衝突常見的「油漲股跌」模式背道而馳。美伊談判

進展反覆或使股市短期波動，但美國 11 月期中選舉將迫使川普在中東議題作出妥協，市場的通膨疑
慮可望下降，且 AI 題材對科技股的盈餘帶動也料持續，科技類股與台日股市都仍有向上潛力。
債市方面，美伊和談雖正向發展，但因雙方歧見仍存，短期內殖利率料維持高檔震盪。然而，目
前債券殖利率水準明顯高於歷史均值，我們認為站在息收角度，債券仍具長線投資價值。此外，投資
人對美債殖利率曲線陡峭化疑慮短期不會消退，加上長債存續期間偏高。因此，我們仍維持債券投資
聚焦中短天期公債/投資級債的建議。

### Page 2

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
美國股市：市場樂觀預期美伊協議達成，美股再創新高
五月美股聚焦於 AI 產業進展與供應鏈訊息，且投資人樂觀預期美伊有望達成協議，並使荷姆茲
海峽恢復正常通行，推動標普與納指再創歷史新高。指數上揚主要由蘋果、美光、超微半導體、
Alphabet、特斯拉、禮來、輝達、英特爾、思科等科技股帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括 Meta、微
軟、沃爾瑪、萬事達卡、直覺、網飛、埃克森美孚、碩騰、新紀元能源、康菲石油等，市場焦點回歸
AI 贏家與受惠的供應鏈。
美國 5 月製造業 PMI 自上月的 54.5 上升至 55.3，服務業 PMI 自上月的 51.0 下降至 50.9，低於

市場預期的 51.2。企業活動維持溫和擴張，但產業表現分化，其中製造業受到企業補庫存帶動，而
服務業略微放緩，高物價與不確定性持續壓抑消費與企業支出意願。此外，就業市場同樣出現分化，
製造業就業小幅增加，但整體民間企業聘僱增速下降，企業招聘態度趨於保守。價格方面，供應鏈延
誤與能源價格上漲推升企業成本。綜合來看，雖然美國經濟的確具有韌性，但通膨壓力仍持續累積。
美國 5 月密大消費者信心指數顯示，由於高油價與通膨疑慮升溫，消費者信心再創新低。密大消
費者信心指數終值由初值的 48.2 下修至 44.8，現況指標由初值的 47.8 下修至 45.8，預期指標則由
48.5 下滑至 44.1。消費者 1 年期通膨預期上升至 4.8%，且 5-10 年中長期通膨預期則大幅升至 3.9%，
全面高於預期。油價與生活成本壓力持續惡化下，消費者不僅對當前經濟與財務狀況評價惡化，對未
來景氣與通膨前景亦轉趨悲觀。近期 FOMC 會議紀錄顯示部分官員已開始討論移除政策聲明中的寬
鬆傾向指引，反映聯準會內部同樣對通膨風險的警戒升溫，政策立場轉趨偏鷹。

AI 投資熱潮推動標普 500 企業現金配置從庫藏股轉向資本支出。市場共識預估 AI 雲端業者(亞馬
遜、Alphabet、META、微軟、甲骨文)2026 年資本支出可望超過 7,000 億美元，為強化現金流量，
這些公司只好削減庫藏股。近期財報季已突顯由庫藏股轉向資本支出與研發的趨勢，大型科技巨擘除
非選擇大規模舉債，否則未來一年企業庫藏股動能恐難明顯回升，讓未來一年的股市波動性擴大。
目前標普 500 本益比已升至約 26 倍，前瞻本益比也在 22 倍位置，評價面已偏高，加上新任聯
準會主席就任後政策不確定性、美伊談判反覆、AI 主題擁擠度偏高等多重變數，市場拉回風險仍需
留意。然而基本面仍具支撐，故雖然短期需考量估值、通膨與地緣政治風險，但中長期則維持對美股
及 AI 產業正面看法，操作上建議以分批、長期角度布局。

日本股市：AI 題材提振日股表現；日本央行 6 月升息機率上升
五月日股受惠於 AI 產業表現強勁，日經 225 指數站上 6 萬 5 千點歷史新高。市場主要由軟銀、
村田製作所、瑞可利控股、瑞薩電子、鎧俠、東京威力科創、三菱 UFJ 金融、索尼、住友商事等公
司帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括三菱重工、愛德萬測試、藤倉、三井物產、豪雅、丸紅、任天堂、
豐田、Disco、富士通等。市場焦點回歸 AI 主題，以及升息可能帶動的金融股。
日本 4 月出口年增 14.8%。弱勢日圓對出口帶來挹注，而實質出口年增 3.4%、連續兩個月維持

正成長，顯示出口量的成長亦是推動出口上行因素之一。引導出口成長的重要品項包含半導體電子零
組件及非鐵金屬，其中半導體等電子零組件連續八個月的雙位數成長，就是受惠於 AI 產業需求。
日本 4 月 CPI 年增幅由前月的 1.5%下降至 1.4%，扣除新鮮食品的 CPI 年增率則由 1.8%上升至
1.4%，本月通膨的走弱為政策性補貼所影響。其中商品價格年增率上升至 1.7%，服務價格年增率則
降至 0.9%。展望後市，日本政府很可能繼續以財政手段平滑 CPI 波動。首相高市早苗於 5/25 宣布，
為因應長期化的能源成本飆漲，日本政府將編列規模約為 3 兆日圓的追加預算案，實際內容包括汽油
補貼、電力及瓦斯費補貼。高市同時承諾不會增加整體政府借貸，試圖緩解財政壓力憂慮。
貨幣政策方面，市場預期日銀將在 6 月啟動升息。近期日銀委員小枝淳子談話，提及應以適當地
速度調升政策利率以因應物價上漲，認為中東局勢惡化導致物價上漲的風險正在增加，然而小枝淳子
於上月會議認為應維持政策利率不變。先前美國財長貝森特表示，日本經濟基本面強勁，日圓過度波

動並不可取，相信日本央行將會採取必要行動。市場預期日本央行於 6 月啟動升息(下次會議時間為
6/10~6/11)，全年至少有 2 碼升息空間。近期可觀察日本十年期公債殖利率飆升來到 2.7%，對照美
國十年期公債殖利率仍在 4.5%，雙方利差持續縮減，有利日圓守穩 160 關卡。
日本 4 月外國人入境人數年減 5.5%至 369.2 萬人次，中國訪日旅客年減 56.8%至 33.1 萬人，連
續五個月出現雙位數的衰退，反映自去年日中關係惡化，中國遊客數維持於 30 萬人左右區間，但整
體外籍觀光客仍在單月 350~380 萬人次的高檔位置，顯示對日本觀光產業造成的負面影響有限。
日本經濟受惠於 AI 產業擴張，加上日本政府對 17 項戰略產業政策支撐明確，然而股市短期面對
中東衝突不確定性及漲多拉回風險，建議投資人可留意中東情勢、補充預算審議進度及日銀 6 月會議
釋放的政策訊號，把握利空拉回機會、以長線角度、分批佈局日股。

新興亞洲股市：半導體與油價角力，亞洲市場分化表現
亞洲股市 5 月因中東情勢的和緩在科技類股的帶領下反彈，截至 5/26，亞洲不含日本指數
(MXAPJ) 過去一個月上漲 5.59%。南韓股市漲幅居前，KOSPI 指數單月上漲 21.19%。台灣加權指
數表現居次，上漲 12.10%。中國股市表現平平，上證指數上漲 1.78%，國企指數不漲反跌，下跌
2.56%。MSCI 東協指數受惠於美伊情勢和緩，5 月上漲 2.15%，但各國表現不盡相同。原本受惠於
油價上漲的馬來西亞及印尼由漲轉跌，其中印尼股市遭到外資短時間內在股市大舉提款，股匯雙殺，

單月下跌 12.95%，亞股中最差。新加坡、泰國、菲律賓則有 3-6%的漲幅。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌
0.55%，印度作為能源淨進口國，5 月上旬高企的國際油價加劇了市場對其國內通膨的擔憂，使得印
度央行在貨幣政策操作上的降息空間受到壓制，也壓抑了股市。
5 月亞洲製造業呈現以科技出口為核心的兩極分化格局。台灣在 AI 算力與全球半導體供應鏈強
勁拉動下，PMI 飆升至 60.3 領跑全亞洲；南韓則在外需復甦與記憶體晶片需求帶動下展開 V 型反
彈，一路回升至 53.6。印度雖自高檔拉回至 54.3，經濟在高油價的干擾下雖能維持擴張，但卻連續
放緩。東協因歐美消費降溫、通膨壓制，動能連月放緩至 50.7 的榮枯線邊緣；中國大陸則持續在 50.3
微幅擴張處橫盤拉鋸，反映整體內需復甦依舊沉悶。
亞洲經濟呈現典型的「科技強、傳統弱」格局，台、韓、兩國受惠於結構性轉型紅利而氣勢如虹，
雖也共同面臨中東衝突引發的原物料與運費飆升等成本通膨隱憂，但影響有限；相反地，缺乏科技大
潮加持的東協、印度與中國，在全球高利率與需求放緩的環境下，正提早面臨景氣停滯的嚴峻挑戰。

台、韓兩國經濟擴張具韌性，顯示全球算力基礎建設正處於實質出貨期。由於地緣政治與中東情
勢陰霾未散，資金將更傾向流入具有「實質獲利高成長」與「定價能力」的東北亞半導體巨頭。台股
加權指數與南韓 KOSPI 的強勢格局具備基本面支撐，高昂的運費與原物料成本雖是隱憂，但先進製
程與高階記憶體具備將成本轉嫁給歐美科技巨擘的能力。印度 PMI 持續放緩，股市將受高油價壓制，
印度的核心邏輯在於「長線結構性轉型」。未來只要中東地緣情勢未進一步惡化，國際油價能逐步回
穩，印度央行的貨幣政策擔憂將減輕。預期印度股市在震盪調整之後，長線仍有投資機會。中國製造
業 PMI 與股市的橫盤疲軟顯示內需消費信心的提振需要更多時間。未來可能的發展仍維持「以時間
換取空間」的築底格局，指數上漲空間有限。

科技類股：AI 資本支出全線擴散，企業獲利上行週期確立多頭韌性
全球科技板塊在 AI 基礎設施資本支出持續投入的背景下，正經歷結構性的架構轉型與產業鏈整
體獲利及估值上調。從大型雲端 CSP 業者的長期資本支出規劃，到硬體、網通與半導體設備大廠的
板塊效應外溢，均顯示 AI 需求具有實質支撐。儘管總體經濟環境面臨美債殖利率上行與高利率環境
的挑戰，但科技類股目前正處於明確的獲利上行週期，企業獲利的增長預期將有效緩解估值修正壓力。
根據彭博智庫與 S&P Global 的最新預測，全球大型雲端業者在 2026 年的總資本支出預計將達

到 8,200 億美元，較先前預估的 7,500 億美元出現顯著上修。在 2026 至 2030 年的長線格局下，全
球六大領先 CSP 業者的累計 AI 資本支出將接近 5 兆美元。其中，亞馬遜在 2026 年的資本支出預計
以 2,000 億美元位居首位，微軟與 Alphabet 緊隨其後。雖然高額支出在短期內可能對自由現金流造
成暫時性壓抑，但隨著基礎建設規模擴大，預期將帶動獲利自 2028 年起加速成長；亞馬遜亦設定於
2029 年達成 1 兆美元的營收目標。
而 5 月以來的財報數據也都顯示，AI 資本支出除了算力晶片之外，也進一步擴散至連接端與製造
設備端。其中輝達單季營收將挑戰 780 億至 800 億美元。毛利預計將穩定維持在 75%左右的高檔。
思科單季營收年增 12%至 158.4 億美元，淨利年增 35%至 33.7 億美元。受惠於大型雲端業者對基礎
建設的投入，思科將全年 AI 相關訂單預估由原本的 50 億美元大幅上調至 90 億美元。應用材料第 2
季營收達 79.1 億美元，獲利超市場預期，且第 3 季財測達 89.5 億美元。執行長預期 2026 年設備業

務將成長三成以上，這些公司的財報都顯示 AI 投資由算力端向連接端及設備端外溢的趨勢明確，良
好的基本面已經成為科技類股的成長基石。
市場正擔憂債券殖利率走高與高利率環境對科技股估值造成的壓力，但我們認為，應從估值面與
企業獲利週期進行評估。當前的總體經濟與市場結構與 2021 年的緊縮週期有些類似，但存在本質上
的差異。在 2021 年前後，聯準會為快速飆升的通膨而啟動升息，科技股因而下跌，但當時那斯達克
100 指數的預估本益比高達 35 倍，且企業獲利正處於下行週期。而目前那斯達克 100 指數預估本益
比約僅為 25.6 倍低於當時，且企業獲利更明確處於上升週期。短線上，殖利率維持高檔確實會讓科
技股產生技術性的獲利了結與波動，但在長期 AI 資本支出結構性成長與整體企業獲利步入上升週期
的雙重支撐下，高利率環境對科技股長線評價的威脅將逐漸緩解。建議投資人關注整體產業獲利全面
提升的帶來的結構性機會。

台灣股市：先進製程帶動結構性擴張，類股良性輪動構築中長線多頭
台灣 4 月外銷訂單金額達 874.5 億美元，創歷年同月新高，年增率高達 48.1%。受到 AI、高效
能運算及雲端資料服務需求的強勁拉動，其中電子產品與資訊通信產品訂單表現尤為顯著，預期未來
數月外銷訂單可望穩定維持在每月 800 億美元的水準。在供應鏈「去中化」趨勢下，全球高端製造
需求正高度向台灣集中。半導體持續擴產亦帶動本土精密機械與檢測設備訂單增長，動能已逐步外溢
至傳統產業，使整體經濟走向更全面的復甦。
在外銷訂單高度成長的背後，正是因為台積電在先進製程與封裝技術上確立了絕對領先地位帶動

了整個台灣上下游產業鏈。台積電技術論壇揭示，在 AI 驅動下，2030 年全球半導體市場規模將達
1.5 兆美元，其中 AI 將貢獻 55%以上的營收。目前全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）對輝達 Blackwell
架構的強烈需求，高度仰賴台積電先進封裝產能的釋放。台積電的 2 奈米（N2）製成即將量產，更
先進的製程預計於 2026 年下半年投產，並著手規劃埃米世代製程。反觀英特爾與三星目前仍在先進
製程節點與良率之間掙扎，台積電不僅在技術進程上領先一至兩個世代，更透過良率高達 98%的
CoWoS 先進封裝建立技術壁壘，短期內對手在技術成熟度、量產規模與客戶信任度上皆難以競爭。
基本面的強勁表現反映在台股的籌碼與盤勢結構上。隨著 5 月 21 日輝達財報化解市場觀望心理，
三大法人全面翻多，單日合計買超達 850.51 億元，其中外資單日買超即佔了 603.41 億元，成交值
顯著放大。此波修正成功洗淨市場的短線浮額。資金從漲多的記憶體板塊撤出，流入基期較低的面板、
載板、被動元件等產業，盤面呈現健康且偏多的類股輪動特徵。資金不再僅僅獨攬於少數 AI 權值股，

而是向各受惠供應鏈上下游擴散，構築了多頭的廣度。
在地緣政治上，5 月川習會整體會談並無重大協議，中美脫鉤的大趨勢持續。中美雙方在台灣問
題上維持各自表述，中方警告「台獨」與台海和平水火不容；美方則重申對台政策維持不變，雙方沒
有重大改變。在全球 CSP 廠商對算力需求尚未觸頂的基本面下，台灣高端製造與半導體供應鏈的核
心地位，並未受到政治會談結果的實質干擾。輝達財報行情與訂單表現依然反映出算力稀缺的市場共
識，地緣政治風險僅止於情緒面擾動，未動搖產業趨勢。 台灣在全球 AI 與高效能運算資本支出浪潮
中，憑藉半導體先進製程與封裝技術，展現出極強的定價權與不可替代性。這使得總體經濟指標表現
亮眼，且擴張動能正由科技業外溢至傳統產業。台股呈現健康的類股輪動格局，地緣政治政治雜音並
未動搖高端製造向台灣集中的趨勢，中長線維持看多格局。

拉美市場：巴西總統大選前料將震盪，後市中性看待
5 月以來拉美股市表現相對疲弱，主因隨著 10 月巴西總統大選逐步逼近，選情不確定性升溫，
導致最大經濟體巴西股市出現震盪，同時美伊和談狀況膠著，油價持續維持高檔，帶動整體拉美國家
通膨預期升溫，降息預期縮窄，股市上漲動能便受到壓抑。
回顧歷史，巴西股市於大選前半年走勢普遍不佳，過去五次選舉前六個月 MSCI 巴西指數報酬中
位數為負 4%，其中 2014 年與 2022 年兩次大選年更以全年下跌作收，顯示選情雜音升高之際，投
資人多於投票前轉趨觀望。在大選過後，巴西股市報酬率則取決於是否由資本市場偏好的候選人當

選，若是，則股市大多上漲，若否，則股市大多下跌，巴西股市 5 月來的跌幅就與此相關。
本次巴西總統大選中，相對受到資本市場偏好的候選人波索納洛，近期捲入醜聞，導致其支持度
大幅滑落，其被指控與涉案巴西史上最大銀行詐欺案 Banco Master 的前執行長關係密切，並涉及收
受其資金用於拍攝家族紀錄片。受此影響，民調機構 AtlasIntel 5 月 19 日最新民調顯示，在第二輪
對決調查中，魯拉以 48.9%支持度領先波索納洛的 41.8%，波索納洛由原先小幅領先轉為落後約 7
個百分點。由於市場對偏左派的魯拉財政與政策路線疑慮較深，民調再度倒向魯拉，連帶導致巴西股
市表現承壓。
另一方面，受美伊戰爭影響，西德州原油價格持續維持高檔，帶動巴西輸入性通膨壓力上升，市
場對通膨回落的信心隨之轉弱。根據巴西央行截至 5 月 22 日的 Focus 市場調查，市場預期 2026 年
消費者物價指數預期中位數已由四週前的 4.86% 升至 5.04%，進一步突破 4.50%的通膨目標上緣；
受此影響，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 基準利率的預期亦由四週前的 13.00%上修至 13.25%，意味市場

預期下半年降息幅度僅約 1 個百分點，降息預期幅度持續收窄，連帶限制股市評價向上修正的空間。
然而儘管受到選舉及通膨升溫兩股承壓力量，高盛最新報告仍對於巴西股市中長線維持加碼評
等，看多理由在於，巴西股市對本地利率的負相關程度為新興市場中之最，在過去降息循環中，股市
於首次降息後往往出現強勁漲勢，而目前巴西市場仍處於降息循環中；此外，今年以來外資對巴西股
市淨流入居新興市場之冠，亦有利股市支撐。
展望後市，降息循環有利巴西股市支撐，然而考量巴西股市於大選前表現普遍不佳，同時油價高
檔推升通膨預期、降息幅度持續收窄的情況下，指數上檔空間恐受壓抑，故我們對於拉美市場後市目
前抱持中性看法。

能源類股：雖仍供不應求，惟油價料將隨談判進度震盪走低
當前油價與能源類股表現仍取決於美伊和談進展， 5 月 23 日川普宣稱一份結束戰事並重啟荷
莫茲海峽的備忘錄已大致談定，將採先確立大方向原則，後續再就細節分軌協商的模式來結束戰爭，
伊朗官媒指出，荷莫茲海峽通行與核子相關議題，將分別在備忘錄簽署後 30 天及 60 天進行談判，
談判期間美國將分階段豁免伊朗制裁。然而截至最新消息，美伊雙方在荷莫茲海峽主權與通行收費、
鈾濃縮是否完全停止等核心議題上仍存在明顯分歧，故該份備忘錄能否順利簽署仍具不確定性。
受到荷莫茲海峽持續封鎖影響，IEA（國際能源署）5 月月報指出，4 月全球石油供給量再度下

降每日 180 萬桶至每日 9,510 萬桶，下降主因波斯灣國家停產量持續上升，不過美國、巴西、加
拿大等國家持續增加出口，也為全球油市提供部分緩解，IEA 將 2026 年美洲供給成長上修逾每日
60 萬桶至平均每日 150 萬桶，大西洋盆地原油出口也自 2 月以來增加每日 350 萬桶。
此外全球石油需求量下滑，也部分抵銷供給缺口。IEA 將 2026 年全球石油需求下修至年減每
日 42 萬桶，較開戰前的預測減少了每日 130 萬桶，產業面看以石化與航空產業需求下滑幅度最
深，國家別中，中國 4 月海運原油進口量相較 2 月大幅減少每日 360 萬桶，日韓印亦分別縮減每日
190 萬、100 萬與 76 萬桶。全球石油需求下降，主因中東石油供給受阻，導致部分石化公司被迫
減產，此外部分也跟中國經濟表現疲弱，導致的需求下滑有關。
然而儘管全球石油需求量下滑，非中東地區國家也持續增產，全球石油市場仍面臨每日約 750 萬
桶的供給缺口，受此影響， IEA 統計全球可見庫存 3 月減少 1.29 億桶，4 月再減 1.17 億桶，

高盛則預估 5 月以來全球可見石油庫存以每日 870 萬桶的創紀錄速度被消耗。
但值得注意的是，儘管全球石油市場面臨嚴重供不應求，理應有利油價上漲，然而整個 5 月西
德州原油價格卻仍維持在每桶 80 至 110 美元區間震盪，並未進一步上漲。高盛指出，油價上漲受
阻，主因經濟合作暨發展組織(OECD)國家以每日 130 萬桶速度釋出戰略石油儲備，填補了部分缺
口，同時中國原油進口量自 2 月 27 日以來大減每日 430 萬桶，使市場上對於可售原油的爭奪趨
緩，連帶使油價漲幅受到受抑。
展望後市油價，EIA（美國能源資訊署）預估布蘭特原油價格 5、6 月均價將約為 106 美元，
隨後續海峽通行恢復，第四季油價可望回落至每桶 89 美元，惟若海峽重開延後至六月底，則後續
油價將可能較此預測在上升每桶 20 美元。整體而言，後市油價及能源類股表現，仍將視美伊談判
進度而定，然而考量美伊戰事持續朝收斂方向前進，預料後續油價將隨談判進度震盪走低，相較不利
能源類股表現，在缺乏進一步利多支撐的狀況下，我們對於能源類股後市目前保守看待。

黃金類股：黃金表現仍承壓，惟全球央行買盤漸強
5 月受到美伊戰爭帶動美債殖利率上行影響，黃金表現仍承壓，然而我們觀察到來自全球央行的
買盤逐漸轉為強勁，顯示各國央行對於逢低增持黃金的興趣仍存，有利為金價帶來支撐，預料來自央
行的買盤將可使金價保持在區間內震盪整理，等待美伊衝突緩解，殖利率上行的壓力消退。
央行買盤方面，高盛於 5 月報告大幅上修其央行購金量預估，主因其推估模型自 2025 年 8 月起
系統性低估各國央行購金量，經參數修正後，高盛預估各國央行購金量 12 個月移動平均值由原本估
計的每月 29 公噸調升至每月 50 公噸，單月修正幅度更大，1 月由 12 公噸上修至 66 公噸、2 月由 2

公噸上修至 41 公噸，2026 全年央行購金量則預估為平均每月 60 公噸。
世界黃金協會數據亦呼應此預估，根據世界黃金協會統計，2026 年第 1 季全球央行淨購金達 244
公噸，季增 17%，超越前季與五年均值。在已申報買方中，波蘭央行 Q1 增持 31 公噸為最大買家，
烏茲別克央行則增持 25 公噸、哈薩克央行 12 公噸緊隨其後。此外中國人行持續增持黃金動作也未
停歇，在 4 月中國人行單月增持 8 公噸黃金，創下 2024 年 12 月以來單月新高，並且是連續第 18
個月增持黃金儲備，帶動其官方持有量上升至 2,322 公噸，占外匯儲備 9%。從以上數據可以看出，
各國央行在近幾個月金價走疲的階段，仍持續加大力度增持黃金儲備。
在黃金 ETF 資金面亦可見到轉折出現的跡象，繼 3 月黃金 ETF 出現顯著資金流出後，4 月全球
實物黃金 ETF 轉為出現 66 億美元的資金流入，所有地區在均呈正向流入，推動全球黃金 ETF 黃金持
有量從 3 月底約 4,092 公噸反彈 45 公噸至 4,137 公噸，達到歷史第三高，僅略低於 2/27 創下的 4,176

公噸紀錄，其中歐洲基金表現最強，4 月流入 37 億美元使年初至今累計由負轉正，主要受地緣政治
風險升溫、英國央行立場不如預期鷹派及本地股市回跌驅動。亞洲則延續第 8 個月淨流入趨勢，4 月
增加 18 億美元，其中香港受惠於新 ETF 產品掛牌上市錄得 7.32 億美元單月歷史新高，中國在地緣
緊張、殖利率下行及官方持續宣布增持黃金的背景下亦流入 4.98 億美元。由黃金 ETF 數據亦可看出，
市場資金對於逢低增持黃金配置的興趣正在上升。
展望後市，在國際地緣政治紛擾不斷的狀況下，仍可預期央行買盤與個人投資組合中分散風險的
需求可望延續，有利為金價帶來支撐，然而在高油價將可能使利率維持在高檔較久的環境下，黃金
ETF 資金流入恐難以重現 2025 年的強度，故預期中短線上金價將可能持續維持在區間內震盪，惟若
後續美伊談判推展順利，隨荷莫茲海峽開放，油價回落將有利市場降息預期回升，屆時搭配來自央行
的買盤，將有望再度重啟黃金新一輪多頭走勢。

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：市場觀望華許首秀，美債利率料陷整理走勢
自 2/28 美伊戰事爆發並導致荷莫茲海峽封鎖以來，全球金融市場經歷自 1970 年代以來最嚴重
的供給端通膨衝擊。西德州原油與布蘭特原油價格的大幅飆升，引發市場對於通膨，乃至於 Fed 利
率政策方向轉變的疑慮，成為推升美債殖利率的核心驅動引擎。
這場由地緣政治引發的能源危機，並非僅侷限於美國單一國家，而是全球公債市場的普遍現象。
由於全球能源報價以美元計價，且原油實體供給短缺屬於全球性危機，主要已開發國家的公債殖利率
呈現極高相關性的同步上揚，其中又以日本與英國的公債殖利率升幅最大。日本 75% 能源依賴中東

供給，不論在物價上或是財政體質上面臨不小壓力。
我們預期 6 月市場的總經焦點將在華許首次主持的 6/17 FOMC 利率會議。4 月下旬在分析華許
在參議院聽證會的發言時，便已提出其主張可能導致「美債殖利率陡峭化」與「債市波動性恐將加大」
的想法。彭博 4/30~5/13 調查顯示，118 名受訪者中有 64% 預期華許上任後，美債利率將出現某
種形式的陡峭化，其中熊市陡峭化（長率升速>短率升速）是主流觀點(佔比近 30%)。未來若債券殖
利率曲線呈現陡峭化(不論是熊市、牛市或是扭轉陡峭化)，短中天期的債券殖利率表現都將相對優於
長天期債券，加上短天期債券的存續期間本就低於長天期公債，因此當債市震盪時，短天期債券價格
表現將優於長天期債券。
巴克萊近期研究也顯示，當聯邦基金利率在 2-5% 的區間內波動，平均約為 3.6% 時，30 年
期公債殖利率與聯邦基金利率之間的利差為 170 個基點。假設長期中性利率在 3-4%的區間內（如
聯準會點陣圖所示），30 年期公債殖利率應在 4.7-5.7%區間。而目前約 5%水準約在區間相對偏低

的水準。巴克萊認為，在美國經濟韌性的背景下，目前 30 年期公債殖利率還不算高，考慮到當前的
財政狀況和異常的不確定性，投資人可能需要更高的期限溢酬。若此說法成真，相較於短期債券，長
天期債券殖利率上升幅度更高(價格下跌幅度更大)。
雖然 5 月至今股市有所表現，但在中東地緣政治談判反覆，能源通膨預期以及聯準會偏鷹派立場
的交互牽引下，債券市場表現卻相對震盪，30 年期美債殖利率甚至一度觸及 2007 年以來的高位。
近來市場定價邏輯高度聚焦於「通膨風險與聯準會政策路徑」，債券殖利率 6 月仍將持續面臨高檔震
盪的壓力。目前債券殖利率水準明顯高於其歷史均值，我們認為站在息收(Carry)的角度，債券仍具
長線投資價值。此外，由於預期美債殖利率曲線趨向陡峭化，再加上長天期公債本就有較高的存續期
間，故就債券價格來看，短天期公債/投資級債券的表現料將優於長天期公債/投資級債券，因此我們
仍維持債券投資聚焦中短天期公債/投資級債的建議。

## Document 173: 261112_daiwa_accton

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Accton’s 3Q26 results, released on 12 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat/miss expectations. Accton’s 3Q26 
net profit came in at TWD13.1bn (EPS: TWD23.3), in line with our estimate 
(TWD13.0bn) but 6% lower than the Bloomberg consensus (TWD13.9bn).  
Revenue was TWD120.7bn (+26% QoQ, 66% YoY), higher than our/BBG 
estimates by 13%/2%, mainly due to increasing AI card contribution for 
Trainium 3. Nonetheless, gross margin fell by 2.1pp QoQ to 17.6% (vs. our 
19.1% estimate), reflecting rising material costs (especially from memory). 
Consequently, OPM declined by 1.6pp to 13.4% (vs. our 15.1% estimate), 
which was slightly offset by better operating leverage.

Business outlook. We believe gross margin is likely the key highlight of 
the 3Q26 results. Per management, gross margin was diluted due to rising 
memory costs. As such, Accton has shifted its business model from “Buy-&-
Sell” to consignment from September. Since then, we see gross margin 
gradually recovering back to the 20% level. Looking into 4Q26, we believe 
Accton could sustain the gross margin, but the networking chipset shortage 
might weigh on revenue momentum in the short term. The alternative is 
that one of the key switch clients could shift its orders slightly towards 
Spectrum first to ease the near-term shortage issue, in our view. As for the 
AI card business, we believe the total shipment has been largely stable 
since 3Q26. Even when the end-client gradually shifts its product mix from 
air-cooled to the liquid-cooled version, we believe the rising content from 
the liquid-cooled version should be able to offset the slightly lower ASP per 
chipset given there are more chipsets (4x vs. 2x from air-cooled) on an 
ASIC AI motherboard. Looking into 2027, we maintain our original view that 
total AMZN ASIC units will grow c.30% YoY to c.3m as the first growth 
drivers. On top of this, the liquid-cooled version has adopted the PCIE 
scale-up switch, which can expand TAM for Accton, in our view. Lastly, for 
the top-of-rack (ToR) switches, we expect 800G switches to remain the 
mainstream models in 2027 but with rising enterprise usage, which could 
likely be the next growth driver in 2027.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 6-12% to factor in 
higher ToR switch revenue. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating, but lower our 
12M TP to TWD3,000 (prior: TWD3,666), based on a target PER multiple of 
24x (prior: 35x, within its past-3-year range of 10-33x), applied to our 1-
year forward EPS forecast, to reflect the weaker market sentiment. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected ASIC server and switch demand.

How we differ: Our 2027E EPS is 5% higher than the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely due to our more positive view on operating leverage.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: gross margin dilution on memory costs

➢ Fundamentals largely on track with our prior estimates  
➢ Recent pullbacks provide good entry points to build positions 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12M TP to TWD3,000

Company profile 
Founded in 1988, Accton is dedicated to the design and manufacturing of enterprise networking and 
communication products. With its long-term experience and expertise, Accton is one of the leading 
open-source networking ODM partners for major players worldwide, with particular focuses on data 
centres, telco carriers and campus networks. Major products include enterprise switches, metro access 
switches, network applications and wireless products.

### Page 5

5

Accton Technology (2345 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Accton’s board has 7 directors, with 3 independent directors. The independent directors account
for 43% of all directors. A total of 8 board meetings were held in 2025, and the average actual
attendance rate was 95%.
The backgrounds of independent directors are diversified and their competence includes
business management, accounting, finance, and work experience in the medical industry. We
deem the diversified background as a positive factor for the company’s board quality.
Besides, the Chairman and President are not the same person, which increases the board and
executive management’s quality. Based on its board structure and key executive hiring, we
believe that Accton has already professionalised the family business by allowing external
professionals to run the business for major shareholders.
Capital management 1
Accton declared DPS of TWD15.0 in 2025, representing a 31.8% payout ratio. The ratio was
lower than the 51.2% in 2024 and its historical range of 69-75% from 2018 to 2022. That said, it
still points towards a friendly shareholder dividend policy, in our view.
We attribute the lower payout ratio mainly to the sharp earnings growth and continued capacity
investments in Taiwan and Vietnam amid US-China tensions. The DPS CAGR in the past 10
years was c.23%, which showcased Accton’s capability in capital management. The continuous
improvement in networking capabilities (eg, 400G and 800G switches), alongside expansion into
AI accelerator modules, has helped it differentiate itself from other competitors and strengthen its
position in the white-box switch supply chain for major US cloud service providers.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, revenue from related parties accounted for 0.15% of total revenue, while purchases
from related parties accounted for 2.0% of cost of sales. We see limited risk from related-party
transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
Like most vendors, Accton asks its suppliers to follow: 1) the hazard substance requirements of
the RoHS, Reach and corporate social responsibilities (5 categories, including labour, health and
safety, environmental, ethics and management system) required by the RBA Code of Conduct,
and 2) QC08000 (Hazardous Substances Process Management), while the company conducts
annual reviews to evaluate its suppliers’ performance.
To effectively track, communicate and manage its supply chain to manufacture green products,
each supplier can use the eGreen system® platform, developed independently by Accton, to
learn about environmental protection control and management requirements for components and
materials.
S Data security 2
To ensure information security for customers and the company, Accton obtained ISO/IEC
27001:2013 certification in 2016 and completed the transition to ISO/IEC 27001:2022 in 2024,
with continued verification of its information security policies and controls in 2025. Apart from the
global information security standards, Accton also takes measures to improve its information
security, including:1) Implementing business continuity and disaster recovery mechanisms,
including annual recovery drills for critical systems and databases. In 2025, the company
achieved its target of recovering critical systems within 48 hours. 2) Conducting monthly
vulnerability scans across company-wide servers, network equipment and core systems to
identify potential security risks. Accton also conducts quarterly social engineering drills to
strengthen employee awareness of cybersecurity threats.
In 2025, Accton conducted four phishing simulation exercises involving over 13,000 participant
attendances, achieving an average phishing safe rate of 93%. The company reported no major
information security incidents or personal data breaches during the year.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

## Document 199: 261116_daiwa_MPI

Source PDF: all report pdf/261116_daiwa_MPI.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: MPI hosted its investor meeting on 16 November. 3Q26 
revenue was TWD5.9bn (+12.8% QoQ), 3.2% below our forecast but 2.1% 
above the Bloomberg consensus. Gross margin rose 3.3pp QoQ to 61.7%, 
beating our/consensus forecasts by 2.2pp, driven by a better product mix. 
Operating margin reached 38.6% (+4.1pp QoQ), 1.4/3.2pp above 
our/consensus forecasts. Management guided for 4Q26E/2027E revenue 
growth of 5-10% QoQ/50% YoY, with gross margin of c.62%/above 60%, 
respectively. We believe the company’s guidance is conservative and 
forecast 4Q26/2027 revenue to be TWD6.7bn/TWD37.7bn, with gross 
margin of 61.8%/61.5% and operating margin of 39.5%/39.9%, 
respectively.

What’s the impact: Probe card capacity expansion on track. MPI 
maintained its capacity expansion plan, targeting monthly VPC/MEMS 
capacities of 2.0m/3.5m probes by end-2026 and 2.5m/6.0m by end-2027. 
Management plans to release its final 2027 expansion plan by end-
December, with potential upside to current targets. Demand remains solid, 
with capacity still insufficient to meet customer needs and no signs of order 
cuts, per management. We believe testing intensity is the key driver of 
sector growth and we expect MPI's diversified customer base to limit the 
impact of order fluctuations from any single customer or project.

CPO contribution likely to accelerate in 2028. Although the market 
seems conservative about its CPO ramp-up, the company claimed that 
Insertion 2 qualification at a tier-1 customer remains on track, with 
qualification results expected by end-December and potential mass 
production orders in 1H27. For Insertion 3, qualification is progressing with 
multiple customers, with potential production orders in 1H27. Given 
qualification lead times, we forecast the CPO testing machine to contribute 
5-13% of revenue over 2027-28, aligned with management’s expectation 
that CPO will contribute a double-digit % in 2028.

What we recommend: We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 6-13% to reflect 
higher opex and cut our 4-quarter-forward target PER multiple from 65x to 
45x to reflect recent macro uncertainties, peer derating and moderating 
earnings growth. As a result, we lower our 12-month TP to TWD5,625 (from 
TWD8,415), while reaffirming our Buy (1) rating. Our target multiple implies 
a c.0.6 PEG based on our 2026-28E EPS CAGR of 73%. Given its 
diversified customer base and continued AI/HPC demand growth, we view 
current levels as offering an attractive risk-reward profile.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 4-5% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more conservative opex assumptions.

16 November 2026

Solid fundamentals; attractive entry post derating

➢ Capacity expansion on track; AI/HPC demand remains intact 
➢ CPO contribution likely to become more significant in 2028  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD5,625

Company profile 
Founded in Hsinchu in 1995, MPI is a leading global probe card supplier offering CPC, VPC and MEMS 
solutions, alongside photonics, semiconductor and thermal testing equipment. Growth is driven by 
expanding AI/HPC demand, rising MEMS adoption, capacity expansion, deeper vertical integration and 
emerging opportunities in CPO testing.

### Page 1

ab 17 November 2026
Global Research
ASPEED Technology Inc
Near-term BMC momentum peaking;
downgrade to Neutral
Three reasons for our downgrade to Neutral
We remain constructive about Aspeed’s growth potential and competitive positioning
longer term, but are downgrading the stock from Buy to Neutral on limited upside vs
expectation in 2027-28. Key considerations: 1) Aspeed’s BMC unit growth is highly tied
to overall Cloud AI TAM expansion, which is at rising risk especially for 2028 after the US

mid-terms and constraint of power / datacenter construction; 2) our analysis shows that
Aspeed may have over-shipped vs end market server units in 2026, leading to
uncertainty of order normalization in 2027 if tight supply eases / demand slows; 3) we
see limited shipment upside in next several quarters from high base in Q426.
Possible order moderation in 2027, after some over-build this year
Aspeed's BMC units grew 78% YoY to reach ~34mn in 2026, of which we estimate

9.5mn units for accelerator servers, and 23.5mn units for general purpose servers. Our
analysis indicates that Aspeed's BMC shipment for general purpose server likely exceeds
the end market server units at ~22.7m this year, implying excess inventory build at
hyperscalers which could cap the upside into 2027. We note that healthy shipment ratio
of Aspeed's BMC units vs end market should be ~80-90% considering Aspeed's 80%
market share, with 5-10% upside for switch, power supply etc. The ratio likely rises to

104% in 2026 from 70%+ in 2024-25. See Figure 1-3 for our analysis.   We lower 2027
BMC units from 48.2mn to 42.4mn, assuming the BMC shipment to reach near term
peak of 11-12m/Q in Q426-Q127, and to soften moderately to ~10m/Q in H227. We
still consider robust growth of AI accelerater BMCs at 82% in 2027E vs 76% in 2026E.
Product roadmap reinforces long-term leadership
Longer term, we remain constructive on Aspeed’s strong execution, accelerated product

roadmap, and rising silicon content per server. Aspeed remains highly focused on BMC
development, unlike  many competitors where BMC is one of several priorities. Its
roadmap, including more advanced BMCs such as the 6nm AST2800, enhanced security
features, and adjacent products such as SMC, I/O expanders and FPGA integration,
should reinforce its leading position in the Cloud AI market over the next 3-5 years. See
our view on Marvell’s infrastructure management controller (IMC) introduction.

Valuation: D/G to Neutral from Buy; lower target to NT$10,000 from NT$19,000
We trim 2027/28E EPS by 14%/11%. Our new estimates are 10/12% below VA
consensus. Aspeed's order outlook remains robust for 2027, but we see risk of
moderation in coming quarters. Downgrade to Neutral from Buy. Our new target of NT
$10,000 is based on 30x 2027E PE, as we roll back from 43x 2027-28E PE given the
rising uncertainty. 30x PE is below Aspeed's historical mid-cycle of 36x, but we view

reasonable considering the potential demand slowdown and 30% LT earnings CAGR.

### Page 2

ASPEED Technology Inc UBS Research
Thesis MapUBS Research THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Can BMC units continue to grow in the next two years after rising 78% in 2026E?
Although we remain constructive about Aspeed's long term execution and technology leadership,
we see a possible order moderation into 2027 after over-build in 2026E. We estimate that BMC
shipment will rise 78% YoY in 2026E and reach around 34m total BMC shipment units with 23.5m

dedicated for general purpose servers—this suggests potential pre-building of BMC inventory by
hyperscalers, as end market general purpose server units are only at 22.7m units in 2026E. In
addition, we are also monitoring the risk of AI data center buildout slowdown in 2028 post the recent
US elections and power constraints. We now forecast BMC shipment growth to moderate in
2027E/28E, with 25%/13% YoY unit shipment growth respectively.
Q: Can Aspeed maintain its dominant 70%-plus market share in server BMCs?

Yes. We expect Aspeed to maintain its market share in the next 3-4 years due to 1) We expect
migration to AST2700 to accelerate in 2027 from a mild 2026 ramp up, with new Intel and AMD CPU
platforms as well as key cloud ASIC projects and Nvidia's new Rubin Ultra AI GPU platform in 2027; 2)
Aspeed has enhanced its BMC offering by embedding flexibility to satisfy customer requirements; 3)
Aspeed is expanding its product portfolio to I/O expanders that integrate functionalities built on

FPGA, RoT security chips, and mini-BMCs (SMCs). 4) Aggressive business strategy to build inventory
for hyperscalers since 2025, which we believe should limit opportunity for competitors.
Q: Can Aspeed maintain overall gross margins at a high-60s level?
Yes. We expect Aspeed's GM to remain in the high-60s over the next few years. Drivers include: 1) the
roll-out of the higher-end AST2700 series, which could solidify Aspeed's competitive position and

help it sustain market share; 2) Aspeed managing back-end logistics for AST2700, reducing reliance
on GUC; and 3) Aspeed raising prices (effective Dec 1) for BMCs to pass through cost inflation.
We are constructive for Aspeed's long-term competitive positioning but are downgrading it to
Neutral. Our key considerations: 1) Aspeed’s BMC unit growth is highly tied to overall Cloud AI TAM
expansion, which is at rising risk especially for 2028 after the US mid-terms and constraint of power /

datacenter construction; 2) our analysis shows that Aspeed may have over-shipped vs end market
server units in 2026, leading to uncertainty of order normalization in 2027 if tight supply eases /
demand slows; 3) we see limited shipment upside in next several quarters from high base in Q426.
1) Our analysis indicates that Aspeed's BMC shipment for general purpose server likely exceeds the
end market server units at ~22.7m this year, implying excess inventory build at hyperscalers which

could cap the upside into 2027. 2) The outcome of recent US mid-terms creates higher uncertainty
for new AI data center build-out in 2028.
Aspeed's share price has rallied ~65% YTD (vs Taipei Exchange Index's 11% gain), but declined 54%
from its peak in Sep. The stock is now trading at 29x/22x 2027/28E PE, de-rated from its peak of
around 63x/48x in Sept, suggesting the market is taking a more conservative stance regarding overall
Cloud AI outlook and Aspeed's growth.

Company Description
ASPEED is the world's largest baseboard management controller (BMC) chipset provider.
ASPEED introduced the first generation of server management processors in 2005. It acquired
Emulex Pilot's remote server management chip business in 2016. Besides server management

chipsets, ASPEED also produces PC/AV extension solutions and image processing system-on-
chips (SoCs). In 2023, server management chipsets accounted for 88% of total sales.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We derive our price target for Aspeed from PE valuation methodology relative to its historical
trading range.
We believe key upside risks for Aspeed include: 1) stronger-than-expected server demand,
particularly from hyperscalers and from AI servers with less crowding out of traditional

servers; 2) further market share expansion; 3) faster-than-expected server platform upgrades;
4) faster-than-expected progress in expanding the addressable content within servers.
We believe key downside risks for Aspeed include: 1) weaker-than-expected server demand,
particularly from hyperscalers; 2) market share losses or more intense industry competition; 3)
slower-than-expected server platform upgrades; 4) slower-than-expected progress in
expanding the addressable content within servers.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over the next 12
months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is the one-year local
market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged
by the analyst to indicate that the stock's price target and/or rating may change in the near term, usually in response to an event that

may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have a 12-month investment horizon.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

our coverage
Substrate CCL: Nan Ya New Material (not covered) has started shipping BT
substrate CCL in small volumes, primarily to domestic customers. We believe this is

driven by raw material supply constraints and has positive implications to non-
Japanese substrate CCL suppliers, including Elite Material, as customers diversify
away from incumbent suppliers.
Glass cloth: Nan Ya New Material expects the price trend of 7628-type glass cloth
to normalize from 2Q27 onwards, while thin/super-thin E glass cloth  remains very
under-supplied in CY27, which should provide support to thin E glass cloth pricing
through next year. The implication to Kingboard is therefore mixed.

Copper foil: Nan Ya New Material sees HTE copper foil declining while its demand
for advanced RTF and HVLP copper foils continues to surge, which bodes well for
Co-Tech, which has exited HTE copper foil business to focus on advanced RTF and
HVLP. The company also sources high-end HVLP from Co-Tech.
Customers: Nan Ya New Material mentioned major Chinese PCB vendors are all its
customers, including Shennan, WUS, Shengyi Elec, Founder, Kinwong, etc. For the

overseas market, it is also engaged with ISU, TTM and Unimicron, primarily for North
America-based customers.
Order change? Nan Ya New Material has not seen any server-related order cuts so
far; it noted the PCB/CCL  demand from the China compute market is growing
strongly. This also has positive implications to Chinese PCB/CCL suppliers Shennan
and Shengyi.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

張予柔 yurou.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

貿聯-KY (3665 TT) 
併購綜效超預期，獲利動能再加速

永豐觀點 
併購 Interplex、新富生貢獻雙雙超預期，獲利上修循環啟動。 
 投資評價與建議 
重申買進建議：研究部本次上修貿聯財務預估：(1) Interplex 資料通訊業務
(Datacom Business)營收規模大幅超標：10 月首個完整併表月營收約新台幣 21
億元，大幅優於研究部原先預期之約 13 億元，研究部同步上修其 2026~2028 年
營收與獲利貢獻預估。(2) 新富生月營收規模自年初以來已成長逾一倍，憑藉資料
中心高階光纖跳線的製造與品質優勢，持續拓展新客戶，研究部預估其 2027 年營
收可持續倍增。(3) 產品組合轉佳、營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 毛利率季增 1.8 個
百點至 32.53%，且營業費用率降至約 12%，帶動營益率創 20.78%新高。綜合預
估貿聯 2026-2028 年集團營收分別為 1,074、1,659、2,161 億元(+51%、+55%、
+30%YoY)；稅後淨利 150、267、356 元(+66%、+79%、+33%YoY)，完全稀
釋後 EPS 分別為 75.25、134.07 及 178.24 元。考量 AI 供應鏈遇短期逆風，影響
評價表現，研究部將本益比調整為 18 倍，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027
年及 2028 年之平均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

ESG 評析 
貿聯企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

### Page 2

2

貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：
貿聯-KY 總部設於加州 Fremont，3Q26 產品比重為資訊科技與數據傳輸 48％、
工業用 31％、車用 8％、電器 12％；若以事業別區分，HPC 占 46％、半導體設
備(SPE)占 13％、其他事業占 41％。貿聯主要核心能力，在於高技術及客製化開
發零組件製造，產品開發以多角化及差異化策略，2026/1 完成收購新富生光電
(XFS)，2026/9/23 完成收購 Interplex 集團資料通訊業務(Interplex Datacom)，
產品線由電源與數據連接延伸至光纖跳線、機殼及機櫃等精密機構件，朝 AI 機櫃
次系統解決方案供應商發展。 
3Q26 財報公布，優於市場預期，營收與獲利同創新高，營益率首度突破 20%：
3Q26 營收 278.75 億元(+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY)，毛利率 32.53%，季增 1.8
個百分點；營業利益率 20.78%，季增 3.6 個百分點、年增 1.2 個百分點，為公司
首度突破 20%，獲利表現優於市場預期。成長主要來自 HPC，3Q26 HPC 營收季
增 30%、年增 61%，營收比重由 2Q26 的 42%回升至 46%，其中電源產品為成
長主力，帶動產品組合改善；半導體設備營收亦季增 11%、年增 70%，各事業全
面成長。費用方面，營收規模擴大但營業費用未等比例增加，營業費用率降至約
12%的歷史低點，顯示獲利率改善來自規模效益與費用效率，而非一次性因素，具
備延續性。稅後淨利 42.93 億元(+44.0% QoQ、+63.3% YoY)，稀釋後 EPS 21.51
元，營收、淨利與 EPS 皆創歷史新高；前三季累計 EPS 48.04 元，已超越 2025 全
年之 46.07 元。

### Page 3

3

研究部上修 2026~2028 年營收獲利預估，原因有三： 
1. Interplex 資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)營收提前兩年達標：Interplex
的資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)自 9/23 起併表，3Q26 僅貢獻 7 天營
收；10 月為首個完整併表月，帶動貿聯 10 月營收達 118.46 億元，月增 26.9%、
年增 80.0%，其中 Interplex 貢獻 6,700 萬美元(約新台幣 21 億元)。對照公司
宣布併購時揭露之年營收 3.92 億美元(月平均約 3,300 萬美元)，Interplex 月
營收已倍增，大幅優於市場預期。研究部看好併購綜效將逐步顯現：Interplex
主要供應 AI 伺服器機殼、機櫃等精密機構件，研究部認為其營收較併購時大
幅成長，主因 AI 機櫃出貨量較年初明顯放大，且併入貿聯後，Interplex 可運
用集團的資金、客戶關係與製造資源擴大接單，雙方產品整合後可提供涵蓋配
電、數據連接、機構件與液冷的機櫃次系統方案，單一 AI 機櫃之可供應產值
明顯擴大。研究部本次上修 2027、2028 年 Interplex Datacom Business 為
貿聯帶來的營收預估，分別為 268、308 億元(原預估 183、250 億元)。 
2. 新富生光通訊規模快速放大，營收占比持續提升：新富生(XFS) 專精於光纖互
連產品的設計與製造，主要產品包括單芯／雙芯跳線、光纖尾纖、多芯光纖以
及 MTP/MPO 高密度連接器，月營收規模自 2026 年初以來成長逾一倍，獲
利能力同步改善，展現規模化高精密光學互連業務的效益。資料中心對光纖組
件的品質與一致性要求遠高於一般應用，新富生在高階光纖跳線的製造經驗與
品質優勢，加上貿聯集團的客戶網絡與全球產能布局，有助其持續拓展新客戶。
隨 AI 資料中心機櫃間光纖連接需求成長，研究部預估新富生 2027 年營收可
望再翻倍增長，且其獲利率改善亦有助提升貿聯整體獲利結構。 
3. 3Q26 營益率創新高，營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 營業費用率約 12%，較 1H26
的 13.7%明顯下降，顯示貿聯在承接更大規模、更複雜的客戶專案時，製造、
工程與行政職能的效率同步提升，費用無須等比例增加。4Q26 起 Interplex
完整併表，營收規模再擴大，研究部預期營益率改善幅度可望大於毛利率，稅
後淨利成長幅度將高於營收。 
HPC 電源成長最強，新平台升級為下一波動能：3Q26 HPC 營收年增 61%、季增
30%，前三季累計年增 77%。隨運算功耗提升，電源產品切入規格更高的新一代
平台；數據連接方面，貿聯同時布局銅纜與光纖，因應不同傳輸距離與頻寬需求。
貿聯於 10 月 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出適用於 OCP 及 NVIDIA MGX 機櫃
之 800V/400VDC 高功率電源方案，以及 1.6T 液冷交換器 OSFP 連接器、機櫃管
理控制與漏液偵測系統，產品範圍由電源線材延伸至配電、液冷與監控。研究部認
為，隨 Vera Rubin 平台放量及 HVDC 架構導入，單一機櫃的電源產品規格與產值
將持續提升，為 2027 年 HPC 成長主軸。 
次世代光通訊布局：貿聯 2026 年先後於 OFC 展示 Optical Shuffle、於 Computex
展示 CPO 交換器托盤方案；9 月於 ECOC 2026 展示與 ams OSRAM 合作之
microVCSEL 光學互連方案，採用 850nm 二維可定址 microVCSEL 陣列，以多通
道、低速率(wide-and-slow)架構降低功耗與延遲，貿聯負責光纖陣列組裝、光學
封裝、光學測試與量產製造；10 月於 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出 CPO、
800G/1.6T 及 XPO 光收發模組、光纖陣列(FAU)與多芯光纖連接等方案。公司表
示產業未來可能並行採用多種光學架構，貿聯以製造與整合能力參與各技術路線，
不需押注單一方案。 
半導體設備成長幅度為產業兩倍以上：3Q26 半導體設備(SPE)營收年增 70%、季
增 11%，營收占比由 3Q25 的 11%提升至 13%，前三季累計年增 48%。根據國
際半導體產業協會（SEMI）於 2026 年 7 月公布之預測，全球前段晶圓製造設備

### Page 4

4

(WFE)市場 2026 年預估年增 23.1%至 1,439 億美元，貿聯 SPE 成長幅度約為產業
的兩倍；自 2023 年以來貿聯之 SPE 業務成長即大幅超越整體 WFE 市場，超額成
長主因貿聯在客戶端的角色由供應線材零組件，擴大至高階組裝、模組與次系統製
造、測試及系統整合，應用以薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備為主，微影與檢測設備為輔；隨
設備製程日益複雜，客戶傾向將更多製造工作委外，以集中資源於核心技術，貿聯
單一設備平台之內含價值隨之提升。SEMI 預估 WFE 市場 2027 年及 2028 年將分
別成長 21.8%及 14.1%，2028 年規模可望突破 2,000 億美元，主要由先進邏輯、
HBM 等先進記憶體投資帶動；公司亦表示，許多客戶對設備投資的強勁預期延伸
至 2030 年以後，且客戶正主動協助貿聯擴充產能，以支援其自身長期成長。貿聯
在 SPE 領域的投資以擴充製造產能為主，Interplex 的精密機械加工與次系統整合
能力亦可與 SPE 業務互補，為 HPC 以外的第二成長引擎。 
隨伺服器架構升級驅動產值倍增，2027~2028 年獲利成長路徑清晰，重申買進： 
研究部認為，貿聯本次財報的關鍵不只是單季獲利優於預期，而是兩項併購同時優
於預期，帶動營收基期結構性墊高：10 月營收 118.46 億元，較 3Q26 月平均約
93 億元高出約 27%。在 AI 機櫃同時需要電源、數據傳輸(銅纜與光纖)與精密機構
件的趨勢下，貿聯透過 Interplex 與新富生補齊產品版圖，單一機櫃可供應產值持
續擴大；而未來一年內，Interplex 完整併表(4Q26)、新富生營收比重提升(2027)、
Vera Rubin 平台與 HVDC 電 源 放 量 (2027~2028) 及 次 世 代 光 互 連 產 品
(2H27~2028)等成長催化劑將陸續到位，獲利上修動能明確。 
評價方面，短期因市場擔憂 GPU 產品推出節奏可能放緩，台灣 AI 供應鏈評價短
期受到壓抑。研究部認為，CSP 資本支出與現行平台訂單短期不受影響，且貿聯的
客戶與產品布局相對分散：公司採取平台中立策略，產品同時供應 GPU 與 ASIC
架構之 AI 機櫃，並服務多家雲端服務業者；銅纜與光纖、電源與機構件等產品線
亦不依賴單一技術路線；此外，半導體設備與其他事業合計仍占營收逾五成，營運
受單一平台影響的程度低於純 GPU 供應鏈。惟考量主要客戶經營層異動可能影響
新平台推出節奏，研究部於評價上給予折價，本益比由前次之 25 倍調整為 18 倍。
搭配本次上修後之 EPS，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027 年及 2028 年之平
均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

## Document 357: Double Feature_ The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love

Source PDF: all report pdf/Double Feature_ The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Double Feature: The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love
Date:    8 hours ago
Double Feature: The Spy Who Loved Me & To China With Love

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Double Feature: The Spy Who
Loved Me & To China With Love
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 12 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Vows on the Move / Unsplash

I. Jensen Versus The AI Trade
Charlie Munger famously warned that there are three ways smart men can
go broke: "Liquor, ladies, and leverage." NVIDIA co-founder and now
former CEO Jensen Huang has run into trouble with the second of
Munger's three L's. He won't go broke, but he is out of a good job.
On Thursday, NVIDIA announced that Huang had resigned as CEO after
33 years at the helm of the company he co-founded in 1993. His

### Page 2

departure followed revelations of extramarital affairs, including a reported
relationship with a Chinese businesswoman suspected by US intelligence
officials of spying for Beijing. Her Singapore-based company, Megaspeed,
allegedly purchased NVIDIA's high-powered GPU chips and re-exported
them to China, violating US export restrictions. The longtime CFO Colette
Kress was appointed interim CEO while the board searches for a
successor.
Huang's departure rattled the AI trade, with NVIDIA shares plunging 4.3%,

sending its forward P/E to 11.7 (charts). We promised you buying
opportunities after the midterm elections. There were a bunch last
Thursday and a few more today.

### Page 3

Competitors AMD and Broadcom advanced 5.9% and 5.5%, respectively,
on expectations of market-share gains (chart)
As a result, the Nasdaq Composite held up relatively well, falling just 0.4%
today (chart).
Neoclouds also tumbled, with CoreWeave down 6.7% and Nebius down
16.8%, amid concerns about NVIDIA-backed financing (chart). Nebius
was additionally pressured by disappointing revenue guidance and rising
capital spending.

### Page 4

Despite the latest AI jitters, the two-year Treasury yield rose 15bps to
4.87% this evening, remaining well above the federal funds rate and
signaling that further Fed tightening is warranted (chart). The 10-year
Treasury yield is back above 5.00% at 5.13% this evening.
Yields rose on higher oil prices, with Brent crude up 3.1% to $95.66 this
evening, after President Donald Trump dismissed the need for an Iran
deal, and on hawkish Fed commentary following October's hot CPI report

(chart).

### Page 5

Markets now price a 100% probability of a December Fed rate hike:
84.7% for 25bps and 15.3% for 50bps. Futures imply more than 3 rate
hikes over the next 12 months (chart).
The combination of higher Treasury yields, driven by rising oil prices and
hawkish Fed commentary, and renewed AI jitters weighed on the broader
stock market. The S&P 500 fell 0.4%, its equal-weighted counterpart
declined 0.9%, and the Russell 2000 lost 1.7% (charts).

### Page 6

II. Nvidia: The Bear Case
We can assess the risks Jensen Huang's departure poses to the broader
AI trade with our familiar framework of three constraints on the AI
investment boom.
(1) Financial constraints. NVIDIA has been described by many as the
central bank of the AI trade, conducting its own version of quantitative
easing by helping finance the expansion of AI computing capacity. The
company spent $42.2 billion purchasing equity securities during the first

half of its current fiscal year, compared with just $1.2 billion a year earlier.
The fear is that Huang's departure could slow or even end these financing
activities, tightening financial conditions for an industry already facing
questions about the returns on its enormous capital expenditures.

### Page 7

(2) Political constraints. Huang has also been the AI trade's biggest
cheerleader, tirelessly promoting its economic benefits and defending key
players. During October's AI jitters, for example, he publicly defended
CoreWeave's financial health amid a selloff in its shares and bonds,
helping restore confidence. His departure could deprive the industry of its
most influential advocate and cheerleader.
Additionally, NVIDIA reportedly intervened in the Commerce Department's

investigation into Megaspeed, while Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick
reportedly resisted efforts to blacklist the company, which remains off the
Entity List. Reports also suggest the Trump administration knew about
Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy. These allegations
could invite tougher congressional oversight and undermine Trump's
national security argument for building more data centers in the US,
making the AI buildout harder to sell to the public.

(3) Physical constraints. Washington could respond by tightening chip
export controls and closing loopholes that allow Chinese companies to
access NVIDIA-powered computing capacity remotely. If so, China might
retaliate by restricting exports of rare earth minerals, some of which are
crucial for building data centers. China has already warned Washington
against using the controversy as a pretext for imposing tougher
technology restrictions.
III. Nvidia: Our Base Case

Our base case is that NVIDIA's financing strategy remains intact. Colette
Kress, NVIDIA's longtime CFO and newly appointed interim CEO, has
worked closely with Huang since 2013 and helped oversee the company's
strategic investments and financing arrangements. During NVIDIA's
August 26 earnings call, she defended these investments against
accusations of circular financing, stating, "We recognize the scale of this
support, and we know some will call it circular financing. We see it

differently." She argued that these investments would generate attractive
returns while strengthening NVIDIA's AI ecosystem, suggesting continuity
under her leadership. We don't expect political constraints to meaningfully
increase as a result of the controversy. Nor do we expect another trade
conflict with China to erupt.

### Page 8

Even if some of the constraints intensify, they would likely turn existing
speed bumps into bigger speed bumps rather than roadblocks to the AI
investment boom. AI remains the latest evolution in the Digital Revolution
that began with IBM's mainframes, and we expect the investment boom to
continue without Huang (chart). We therefore view any resulting selloff as
another buying opportunity for the AI trade.

## Document 403: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI  investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). The effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current-account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-
quarter rolling basis) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI-driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky-rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non-oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it should not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly. 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier-model training 
toward enterprise inference. Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data-center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore extend the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or national security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI lacks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U.S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply-
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappoints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
LatAm’s silicon dividend: tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. – October 28, 2026

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
OCTOBER CPI PREVIEW
Inflationary pressure fades as we move through year-end
17 November 2026
Key points
• October CPI to rise 0.41% with the annual 
pace lifting to 4.8%yr. The seasonally 
adjusted CPI lifts 0.7%mth, a small step 
up from the 0.6% increase in September.  
• October Trimmed Mean (TM) is set 
to gain 0.42%mth lifting the annual 
pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr. 
• There is a small downside revision 
from our October 19th estimate for 
the December quarter CPI to 1.2%qtr 
while the TM remains 0.9%qtr.  
• The December quarter will represent the 
peak in Australian inflationary pressure. 
Our March quarter CPI remains 0.4%qtr 
and the TM is unchanged at 0.7%qtr. 
• We now see TM inflation dipping below 
3% by Q3 2027 and be well within the 
RBA’s target band by end 2027 at 2.7%yr.
• Demand remains the larger driver of 
inflation, but the recent pick-up in 
trimmed mean inflation appears to 
have been largely supply-driven.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Inflation accelerated in September...
The CPI rose 0.8% in the month of September, stronger than 
Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the market’s expectation of 
0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr. The 
seasonally adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting 
some positive seasonality in the month. 
Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: auto 
fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday travel & accommodation 
(8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation 
(7.2%mth); and property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There 
were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–3.1%mth); 
household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The more important underlying measure, the Trimmed Mean 
(TM), gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and 
market expectations with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr 
(revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised 
pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this 
series.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second 
consecutive month. The share of the basket running above 
3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, while the share below 2%yr 
rose from 27% to 32%. Meanwhile, our replication of the San 
Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum index moved into 
positive territory after trending higher since April. Around 
14% of the CPI basket, on an expenditure-weighted basis, 
experienced positive inflation shocks in September, driven 
by holiday travel, fuel, and clothing & footwear. This was 
partly offset by negative shocks across 7% of the basket, 
concentrated in food & non-alcoholic beverages. For full 
analysis of the September CPI, see “ Inflation accelerating in 
the run up to Christmas”.
... but is set to peak in October.
Looking ahead to October, early indicators were mixed. The 
NAB business survey suggested firms continued to absorb 
a meaningful share of higher input costs, with purchase cost 
growth edging up to 2.4%qtr (+0.1ppt from September), while 
retail price growth slowed sharply to 0.2%qtr (–0.8ppts), 
partly reflecting some moderation in labour cost growth 
(see here). Meanwhile, consumers’ year-ahead inflation 
expectations rose to 5.3% in October, with this rate holding 
steady in November. Fuel prices provided a more pronounced 
source of inflationary pressure, with retail petrol and diesel 
prices jumping 8.7% and 5.3%, respectively, in October to 
average $2.40/l and $2.82/l as global oil prices approached a 
local peak.
October to mark the peak in inflation
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.4% (0.41% to 2dp), lifting
annual inflation to 4.8%, which we expect to mark the peak.
On a seasonally adjusted basis, we estimate a stronger 0.7%
increase (0.68% to 2dp). For the monthly trimmed mean, we

expect a 0.4% rise (0.42% to 2dp), nudging the annual pace up
to 3.7%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking a 1.0% increase
in October. While conceptually different from the quarterly
trimmed mean, this measure nonetheless provides a useful
guide to near-term underlying inflation momentum. For market
services excluding volatiles & holiday travel, we have pencilled
in a 0.3% rise, lifting annual inflation to 4.0% from 3.8%,
which had marked a ten-month low.

Transport is expected to make the largest contribution
(+0.35ppts), almost entirely reflecting auto fuel (+0.32ppts).
Smaller contributions are expected from clothing & footwear
(+0.06ppts), household goods & services (+0.05ppts), and
meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The main offset comes
from recreation & culture (–0.18ppts), driven entirely by
holiday travel (–0.18ppts), while fruit & vegetables, utilities
and several other components provide only marginal offsets.

Key details for the month of October
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to rise 0.2% in
October, lifting annual inflation to 3.3%. Strength in the month
is concentrated in meals out & takeaway, where we expect
prices to rise 0.6%mth and 4.3%yr. We have allowed for a
greater-than-usual degree of price resetting as businesses
adjust to the card surcharging ban.
Housing inflation is expected to rise 0.1% in October, with the
annual pace increasing to 6.2% reflecting base effects from

electricity. Across the major components:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.2%mth and 3.5%yr, with the
late-September indexation of the Commonwealth Rent
Assistance flowing through more broadly in October. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate is stronger at 0.4%mth.
• New dwelling inflation has been a key source of downside
surprise in recent months. We expect a 0.2%mth rise
in October, which would see the annual pace moderate
to 4.5%. The downturn in established home prices has

deepened into year-end (see here), which should weigh on
new dwelling inflation over the months ahead. Our current
estimates point to a trough of around 1.3%yr by late-2027.
• Maintenance & repair costs are expected rise a solid
0.9%mth, taking annual inflation to 3.5%, while gas & other
household fuels provide the main offset, falling 2.5%mth.
Transport is expected to be the main source of upward
pressure in October, rising 2.9%mth and lifting to 11.6%yr.

This largely reflects an 8.2%mth jump in auto fuel prices
following the rise in global oil prices through the month. Motor
vehicle prices and spare parts are also expected to increase, by
0.4%mth and 1.6%mth respectively.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Recreation & culture is expected to provide the key offset, 
falling 1.5%mth, driven by a 3.0%mth decline in holiday travel. 
We also expect some pull-back across components exposed 
to the AI trade, including audio, visual & computing equipment 
(–0.2%mth) and games, toys & hobbies (–0.3%mth).
Underlying inflation holds momentum
As noted above, our October TM estimate is also 0.4%mth, a step 
up from 0.3% in September taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 
3.6%yr and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 
4.0%yr, the fastest pace in the short history of the series. Rents 
and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
Upside risks to October baseline ...
We view the risks around the October CPI outcome as slightly 
tilted to the upside. Our estimates already allow for a greater 
degree of price resetting in areas such as meals out & takeaway 
and personal and household services, but there is a risk that this 
adjustment proves broader than we have assumed. Increases in 
fuel levy surcharges in early October are another source of upside 
risk, including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post, with 
the latter nearly doubling its surcharge between September and 
October. The key downside risk remains housing, particularly 
new dwelling prices, which have surprised to the downside in 
recent months and could continue to do so as the housing market 
downturn deepens. Trade notifications data also point to some 
further downside risk, with builders on average announcing 
smaller price changes than in prior months.
... but momentum to ease in November ...
We have a preliminary estimate for the November CPI of 
–0.1%mth which will see the annual pace ease back to 4.6%yr. 
Auto fuel moved lower through November to be down –2.1% in 
the month with seasonal falls in garments, health, furniture & 
household appliances and holiday travel offsetting some strength 
in food, housing, communication and financial services. 
Seasonally adjusted the CPI is estimated to lift 0.1% in November, 
highlighting the seasonal weakness in the month, while our TM 
estimate is for a 0.2% lift seeing the annual pace ease a touch to 
3.6%yr while the six month annualised pace drops from 4.2%yr 
to 3.9%yr. This moderation is expected to continue into December 
with a preliminary TM estimate of 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. 
The following page has a table with monthly profiles for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to July 2027. By July 
2027 we now expect an annual pace of 3.3%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI while our estimate for the TM is 3.0%yr. 
More significantly, the six month annualised pace of the TM is 
estimated to be down to 2.7%yr by June 2027, well within the 
RBA’s target band. We have also included tables breaking down 
the monthly components of the CPI through the December and 
March quarters. 
... with December quarter now the peak ...
Our December quarter CPI estimate has been revised down 
slightly to 1.2%qtr, a modest 0.05ppt revision, with the annual 
pace lifting from 3.9%yr to 4.6%yr. The seasonally adjusted

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
CPI gains 1.4% in the December quarter highlighting normally
December is a seasonally soft quarter. Our TM estimate for
Q3 has a small downward revision of 0.05ppt to 0.85% at two
decimal place but this rounds up to our published estimate of
0.9%qtr. For now that suggests downside risk for our TM

estimate as getting the quarter to round to 0.8% is much
easier than getting it to round up to 1.0% . We also note
auto fuel prices falling faster than expected which may not
only result in downside risks to out CPI estimate but also our
TM estimate due to less pass through pressure.
... with inflation moderating through 2027 ...
Westpac’s preliminary March quarter CPI estimate is just 0.4%.
Driving most of this outcome is the 7.7% decline in autofuel

(–0.29ppt) and a 3.8% fall in holiday travel (–0.23ppt) being
offset by rising food (1.0%, 0.18ppt), housing (0.7%, 0.15ppt),
health (2.6%, 0.18ppt) and education (3.5%, 0.17ppt). The
March quarter does not have a lot in the way of net seasonally
with the seasonally adjusted estimate up 0.5%. Our underlying
TM measure of inflation is estimated to rise 0.7% in the March
quarter taking the annual pace down to 3.5% while the six
month annualised pace dips from 3.8%yr to 3.2%yr.

... as a disinflationary impulse kicks in.
We have for some time expected auto fuel prices to peak in
October, 240 cpl for petrol and 282 cpl for diesel, then to
trend lower from there. Our November estimates are currently
sitting on 237 cpl and 279 cpl respectively trending down to
186 cpl for petrol and 196 cpl for diesel by December 2027.
This represents a 12.7% fall in auto fuel through the first half
of 2027 subtracting 0.42ppt off the CPI over the same period

of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the
auto fuels profile please see: “Persistent energy pressures
have further to run”. At this stage we have not adjusted our
Brent forecast in line with the current correction underway
(see the November Market Outlook for more details why) as
we expect this to be a volatile ride. Should the current correc-
tion continue, auto fuel will represent a larger drag on the CPI
and potentially the Trimmed Mean.

Critical to our expected moderation in core inflation is a step
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab-
lished home prices. From a 1.0%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we
see dwelling price increasing by 0.4% in Q4 2026, and then
0.5% in Q1 and 0.4%qtr in Q2 before dropping to 0.2%qtr in
Q3 and Q4. This will see dwelling price inflation in the CPI
drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 2.3%yr by June 2027 then

down to 1.3%yr by December 2027.
At least to the end of this year there is still a high degree of
uncertainty about how much businesses will be able to pass
through upstream cost pressures. The latest NAB business sur-
vey continues to highlight robust input price pressure but also

that firms are struggling to pass on those increases.
For businesses, it is almost a now or never opportunity to raise
prices. We expect that through 2027, as energy prices ease
and the domestic economy slows under the weight of higher
interest rates and falling established home prices, households
will be much more resistant to price increases especially if dis-
cretionary household spending softens by more than expected.
That said, these headwinds may be at least partially offset by

the stronger starting point for household incomes, which has
proven more resilient than previously anticipated, and a possi-
ble bounce back in energy prices due to renewed conflict in the
Middle East.
Supply or demand driven inflation?
Inflation has steadily drifted away from the RBA’s target over
recent quarters. While the demand side is the larger driver of
inflation currently, most of the recent pick-up in TM inflation
appears to be from the supply side, and the more detailed

breakdown may understate how much.
There are several ways to split inflation into supply and de-
mand. One is to estimate how much of the rise a standard de-
mand-driven model can explain and attribute the rest to supply
(see here). Run through to Q3 2026, this approach puts most of
the recent pick-up in TM inflation down to supply. Without it,
trimmed mean would have risen from around 3.0%yr to 3.2%yr
over the year to Q3 2026, rather than the 2.9%yr to 3.7%yr

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
rise we actually saw. The supply contribution is moderate,
though, and well below its Covid-era peak. This analysis also
reveals that most of the disinflation through late-2023 and into
early-2025 reflected supply pressures unwinding, while de-
mand-driven inflation remained stickier, persisting above 3%yr.

Another is the San Francisco Fed approach, which the RBA has
also replicated (see here). The attribution is that if prices rise
and quantities fall, it is supply; if both rise, it is demand. We run
it to Q2 2026, as Q3 volume data are not yet available. Like the
first approach, it finds that demand accounts for most of infla-
tion (headline in this case). However, unlike the first approach,
it shows supply contributions easing a little.
A closer look at the categories classified as demand tells a

more nuanced story. Utilities, health and insurance all feature,
and their prices are largely shaped by government policy or are
not the categories that usually come to mind when people talk
about demand-driven inflation, and they are not very sensitive
to interest rates. You can look at electricity as a clear example
where prices in recent history have been set by government
policies (the introduction of, and then removal of, cost of living
rebates as well as policies to decarbonise the economy) or ex-

ternal supply shock (rising gas prices pushing up the marginal
cost of generation).
Categories more commonly thought of discretionary do show
up on the demand side, with recreation & culture (excluding
travel) and meals out & takeaway being the key ones. The
drawback of this sort of decomposition is that it labels cate-
gories where prices and quantities move together as demand,
even when this may reflect the ripple effects of a supply shock
elsewhere, a point our Chief Economist Luci Ellis detailed in a

note in 2024 (see In a world gone bananas, don’t squash the
other fruit). Some of this may be occurring now. In particular,
the analysis is likely not capturing the shift in spending towards
these categories resulting from travel disruptions associated
with the Middle East conflict (travel services itself was classi-
fied as a supply-side contributor in Q2). As travel normalises,
some of the “demand” contribution from recreation & culture
and meals out & takeaway should therefore fade over coming

quarters.
We would also acknowledge the work of Tim Toohey and his
analysis “The RBA has an identity problem”.
